Getting through a workday is hard, and even basic things at home like folding laundry feel like they take everything I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling, like it’s sitting in my chest. I’ll just sit at the kitchen table and stare out at the yard, and it feels like… I don’t know, like there isn’t really a good version of the future coming.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yeah. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I liked working in my garden or taking photos of birds. Lately I just… don’t. I cancel plans or I leave my camera sitting in the closet because none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say worry is the main thing I notice. I feel tense sometimes, but it’s more like I’m just worn down than actively anxious about something specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you still take pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. Like I said, I used to really enjoy hiking, yoga, being in the garden, taking bird photos… but lately I just cancel or don’t even pick things up. It started around when we took on the mortgage. I haven’t heard voices or seen things that weren’t there. Getting through a workday is hard, and even basic things at home like folding laundry feel like they take everything I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling, like it’s sitting in my chest. I’ll just sit at the kitchen table and stare out at the yard, and it feels like… I don’t know, like there isn’t really a good version of the future coming.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed recently?\n\nPatient: It’s changed, yeah. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I liked working in my garden or taking photos of birds. Lately I just… don’t. I cancel plans or I leave my camera sitting in the closet because none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say worry is the main thing I notice. I feel tense sometimes, but it’s more like I’m just worn down than actively anxious about something specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you still take pleasure in the things you do?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. Like I said, I used to really enjoy hiking, yoga, being in the garden, taking bird photos… but lately I just cancel or don’t even pick things up. My camera’s been sitting in the closet, and it all feels like too much effort for not much reward.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about not wanting to live or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Yes… late at night especially, I’ll catch myself thinking that my husband might be better off without me. I haven’t made a plan or anything, but the thought keeps coming back, and that scares me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About two months, I think. It started around when we took on the mortgage. Before this low stretch, I was in this really overcharged push with work and business stuff, and then it just swung the other way.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange or unusual thoughts, like hearing voices or seeing things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t heard voices or seen things that weren’t there. My thoughts can get pretty dark, but not like… strange in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it, and I don’t think there’s been a big change that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual or feeling like you're speeding up and can't slow down?\n\nPatient: Yes. More slowed down than sped up. My husband has said I move around the house like I’m underwater, and I notice it too sometimes. Even when he asks me something simple, it can take me a while to answer, like the words are coming out really slowly.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes. For more than two weeks, definitely. I used to make time for hiking and yoga, and I loved working in my garden and taking bird photos. Lately I just keep canceling or not going, and my camera’s been sitting in the closet because none of it feels worth the effort.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience any pleasure at all, even briefly?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I can’t feel pleasure at all, ever. But most things I used to enjoy just don’t do much for me lately. Hiking, yoga, my garden, taking bird photos… I keep canceling or just leaving my camera in the closet because it all feels like too much effort and not really worth it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently bad?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty consistently bad. I might have a few moments where I’m distracted, but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling in my chest. I’ll sit at the kitchen table staring out at the yard and just feel like… there isn’t a good version of the future coming.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have. At work I’ll read the same survey results or a client email two or three times and it still doesn’t really sink in. And making small decisions feels hard too, like even picking which spreadsheet to start with can feel weirdly impossible.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to carry out daily responsibilities and take care of yourself most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time I can still do the basics, but it takes a lot out of me. I’m behind on client reports at work, and I’ve been avoiding friends and the outdoor routines that usually help me stay steady. It feels like most of my energy is just going into getting through work and keeping up with basic things at home.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're a burden to your loved ones most days in the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I feel like I’m weighing everyone down. I keep going back over the mortgage decision in my head and telling myself I was selfish and reckless, like I put our whole household at risk. My husband reminds me we made that choice together, but it doesn’t really stop me from feeling guilty about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Not really fidgety, no. If anything it’s the opposite. My husband says I’ve been moving around the house like I’m underwater, really slow. And sometimes when he asks me something simple, it takes me a while to answer because the words just come out slowly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the past two weeks where you felt nearly normal, even if only for a few hours?\n\nPatient: Not really. Maybe there are brief moments where I’m distracted, but most days I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling in my chest. I’ll just sit at the kitchen table looking out at the yard and feel like there isn’t any good version of the future coming.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
